# Consolidated transcription: changes against witnesses C and H

Counts are substantive locations, not tokens. One location can differ from both witnesses. Macro-only variation and certainty-only changes are excluded; doubt decisions are listed separately. Distinct changes within a crowded passage are separated when they can be located independently. The later image-only audit sections also preserve changes in expressed uncertainty; those audit changes enter these witness counts only when the primary reading or another substantive feature differs.

### Unresolved places

- seven d overwrites: \deleted{\unsure{p}}d — Earlier letter may be p or another p/q-like trace; later d is secure.
- blotted superscript above named covering: \deleted{\unsure{q}} — q is only the inherited candidate; the blot does not establish its form.
- fibre line, formée/formé: \unsure{formée|formé} — formé
- canceled word before pts: \deleted{\illegible{1}} — The strike physically obscures the word; earlier points/couples candidates are withdrawn.
- second paragraph, fini: \unsure{\deleted{fini}|fini} — Cancellation leads; live word remains possible.
- between on and désigne: \deleted{\unsure{en}} — Exact short canceled content is uncertain.
- between la and multiplicité: \deleted{\unsure{var.|ens}} — Neither cancelled word is complete beneath the strike.
- after compatible: \unsure{\deleted{avec}|aux} — Cancelled avec leads; live or rewritten aux remains possible.
- after étale: \unsure{à|:} — à / colon; tiny lower body not separable

### Unresolved places

- opening canceled group: \deleted{\unsure{ens|en}} — The terminal hook may be s or part of n under the strike; the initial loop favors e over u. Neither reading is resolved.
- opening valeur ending: valeur — valeurs remains less likely because no distinct final s can be isolated.
- (173) first arrow label: \sigma — Latin s is less consistent with the closed round bowl, but the small size limits certainty.
- (174a) empty-set sign: \phi — \varnothing / \emptyset are the witnesses’ normalized forms; the long projecting stem favors phi.
- (174b) canceled induit: \deleted{\unsure{induit}} — The black cancellation does not permit a secure letter-by-letter reading.
- (174b) right coproduct index: d\in\mathbb N — A dark terminal flourish could conceal an earlier extra mark; no independent additional letter is readable.
- lower marginal parenthesis: \unsure{thm prouvé?|trop fort?} — thm prouvé? is favored; the compressed letters after p are still not secure.
- (174c) source subscript comma: D_{d\unsure{,}*} — D_{d*}; cancellation prevents confirming the comma.
- (174c) lower group: \unsure{\prod|\coprod}\,\unsure{d'|d} — The connecting bar of the two-upright operator and the possible prime after d are both obscured.

### Unresolved places

- a), cancelled superscript on N: \deleted{\unsure{*}} — star is an underlying candidate; overwritten strokes are not individually recoverable
- c), final index of the range: \unsure{d^\circ|d''} — circle; overwritten primes; possible cancellation state of the high cluster unresolved
- cancelled condition, first comparison: d\unsure{\geq|>}d' — weak or strict greater-than; strike obscures a possible lower bar
- cancelled condition, second comparison: d'\unsure{\geq|>}d'' — weak or strict greater-than; strike obscures a possible lower bar
- cancelled triple formula, mark below final cross: \underset{\unsure{?}}{\times} — query-like mark; its semantic or editorial attachment is unknown
- cancelled triple formula, last target subscript: D_{\unsure{d''}} — d double-prime under heavy retracing; previous layer unrecoverable
- first cancelled d), final equivalence sign: \unsure{\sim|\simeq} — sim or simeq; apparent extra horizontal line may be cancellation
- second cancelled d), torsor subscript: \unsure{D^!_{d'}|D^!_{d,d'}} — single d-prime is favoured; crossing strokes obscure exact subscript extent
- isolated lower B/D label: \deleted{$\unsure{D_{d,d'}|B_{d,d'}}$} — D is favored after separating the long cancellation crossbar; B remains possible under the returning upper stroke.
- cancelled token before diagramme cartésien: \deleted{\unsure{rev.|beau|deux}} — The rounded initial body and masked low joins do not securely distinguish the three candidates.
- (176), high mark on first source index: \unsure{d^\circ|d''} — circle; primes; overwritten or cancelled high cluster
- upper marginal note, mark after correspond: \unsure{:|à} — Two separated marks favor a colon; a compressed a beneath an accent is not excluded.
- c), terminal curved mark: \unsure{,|)} — One low curved stroke is visible, not the two marks in both witnesses.
- after donne in the tautological-cover paragraph: \unsure{pour|puis} — The compressed middle joins and small high mark do not securely separate our from uis.

### Unresolved places

- deleted old hypothesis: \unsure{faire|fait} — faire is retained first; fait remains possible under the strike.
- deleted prose relation: \unsure{\text{---}|\longrightarrow} — A short pen turn could be a reduced arrowhead, but a plain line is not excluded.
- first canceled pair of maps: \unsure{\longrightarrow|\text{---}} — Both connector endpoints remain arrow/dash doubtful, especially where the horizontal cancellation overlaps the shaft.
- repeated canceled maps: \unsure{\longrightarrow|\text{---}} — Terminal thickening supports but does not securely prove a head; the vertical simeq signs are clear.
- large canceled diagram, left middle object: D_{\unsure{d,d',0}} — The crowded first two index components and comma/prime are not fully separable.
- large canceled diagram, upper region: \unsure{\mathrm{comm.}|\mathrm{can.}|\mathrm{canon.}} — The competing readings remain visually possible; no label is assigned definitely to the diagonal arrow.
- relative adverb in live paragraph: \unsure{relativ\textsuperscript{t}} — The short form and raised terminal stroke are favored, but the terminal letter remains unclear.
- (178) diagonal connector: diagonal shaft with a small hollow terminal loop, reproduced literally — The loop is visible; whether it was intended as a direction mark remains unresolved.
- (179) top connector: horizontal shaft with a short terminal downward turn, reproduced literally — Whether that turn was intended as a reduced arrowhead is unresolved.
- (179) overwritten layer: \deleted{\unsure{D_0}}\inserted{X} — The live X is clear; only the earlier D0-like layer remains uncertain.
- first category abbreviation in the margin: catég. — Its ending is crowded against the neighboring formula number; the abbreviated extent is clearer than every final small mark.
- cancelled word beneath analytiques: \deleted{\unsure{ordinaire}} — The initial oval, tall medial rise and dot support this tentative word; the ending remains masked by the strike.
- cancelled inserted codimension abbreviation: \unsure{codimens.|codim} — The end of the abbreviation overlaps cancellation strokes; d-d′ itself is clearer.
- cancelled hypothesis noun: \unsure{finité|lissité} — The first loop and central joins are crossed; either recorded word remains possible.
- mark after ailleurs: \unsure{:|à} — Two detached marks favor a colon; a very compressed accented a cannot be excluded.

- final section symbol: \unsure{s_i|s} — The lower-right heavy return can contain a compressed i, but does not isolate it securely.

### Unresolved places

- Second figure label: \unsure{\text{Courbe}} — Courbe is the retained candidate; the overtraced capital is not fully recoverable. No full-word cancellation is established.
- Third figure label, D subscript: D_{\unsure{2|n}}=\phi\ \ldots — 2 | n; neither is silently selected from the mathematical expectation.
- Tail of cancelled block after diviseur sur X: \deleted{Lorsqu'on \unsure{en a choisi|a un \illegible{2}}} — The initial capital is clearer than the continuation. The crossed lower sequence does not establish either en a choisi or a un followed by about two obscured word groups; the second reader’s action is not secure.
- Cancelled word before qui donne naissance: \deleted{\unsure{ou}} — ou is tentative; surviving loops do not recover every letter.
- Final sentence, first fibre invariant: \unsure{\Pi_1|\pi_1} — Π_1 | π_1; the visible form is capital-like, while the conventional interpretation suggests the latter.

### Unresolved places

- Short deletion before syst. local: \deleted{\unsure{rev}.} — rev is a candidate from surviving rises and joins; the crossing prevents certainty about every letter. The point itself is clear.
- Canceled noun after le and below sur D_1: \deleted{le \inserted{sur $D_1$} \unsure{faisceau}.} — faisceau remains the leading candidate, but the obscured middle and ending do not permit an unmarked reading. The article, raised phrase and terminal point are clearer.
- Right end of connector in (185): \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1ex]{\draw (0,0)--(1.8,0)--(1.84,-.2);}} — A reduced rightward arrow is possible; a shaft with an incidental downturned pen finish is also possible. The literal drawing is retained without deciding direction.
- Right end of connector in the D_1 to D_0 hypothesis: D_1\mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1ex]{\draw (0,0)--(1.8,0)--(1.84,-.2);}}D_0 — Reduced rightward arrow versus non-directed connector with a terminal pen turn. The semantic word immersion does not supply a missing head.
- Stroke under the beginning of régulière: \tikz[baseline=(reg122.base)]{\node[inner sep=0pt,outer sep=0pt] (reg122) {régulière};\path (reg122.south west)--(reg122.south east) coordinate[pos=.33] (reg122end);\draw ([xshift=-.2mm]reg122.south west)--(reg122end);} — An intentional partial underline versus an elongated returning g descender. The limited observed extent is certain enough to draw; its purpose is not.
- Final digit of display label following (187): (18\unsure{8|3}) — 8 leads because of the lower loop and upper return; 3 cannot be excluded because the upper portion is compressed. No sequential-number argument is used.
- Raised morphism label in the last paragraph: \unsure{s_i|\delta_i} — s_i is supported by the clearer labels in (185) and (187); the doubled lower loop in this raised occurrence also permits delta_i.

### Unresolved places

- M1263 — abbreviation in (192): \unsure{sys.|syst.} — Sys. is favored; a separate t cannot be secured among the compressed joins.
- Unindexed case uncertainty in (192): \unsure{\Pi_1|\pi_1} — The roof-and-two-legs shape favors capital Pi graphically, but this hand does not securely distinguish it from a large lowercase pi in this occurrence.
- Definition connector: \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-0.5ex,x=1em,y=1em]{\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)--(.9,0)--(.9,-.13);}} — The shaft and downward terminal are copied literally. A reduced rightward arrow is a possible intention; a full arrowhead is not supplied.
- Newly distinguished lower cancellation in the definition: \deleted{\unsure{\text{pour}}} — Pour is a plausible reading of the p-like descender and low bumps. A mere underline/cancellation of the raised D_d would be an alternative physical interpretation, but it fails to explain all lower strokes.
- Blackened unit before plus: \unsure{\deleted{\illegible{1}}} — Possible deliberate cancellation of an unreadable tiny unit, or an incidental pen blot. No readable et is present.
- Final paragraph, compressed word: \unsure{variance|variation} — Variance is favored by one secure i dot and the absence of a secure tall t or second i dot; the compressed suffix prevents a final decision.
- Unindexed C doubt in the lower cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{avec|(193)}} — Avec remains possible; the blind reading (193) is also compatible with some surviving vertical and rounded strokes. Dense cancellation prevents resolution.
- M1265 — left target of the fan: \unsure{D_d^*|D_d} — A small actual high mark is present. Star is the leading interpretation; the alternative leaves it as an unassigned correction remnant rather than pretending that no ink is there.
- Final fan, end of right label: imm. \unsure{local|locale} — A final tall l is visible. Its short exit may or may not include an additional e.
- Relation in the canceled inequality above the live range: \unsure{\geq|>} — The lower bar coincides with the cancellation, so geq versus strict greater-than remains unresolved.

- First D index in second row of (193): d^{\unsure{*}} — Small angular high cluster, perhaps star-like or a retraced prime. It is independently less clear than the first occurrence.
- Upper group label in (195): \mathfrak S_{d^{\unsure{*}}} — The high cluster is star-like; its exact glyph remains open. Neither a bare d nor an unqualified simple prime captures the visible extra strokes.
- Connector of (194): \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1ex]{\draw (0,0)--(1.81,0);\draw (1.8,.15)--(1.82,-.15);}} — Reduced rightward arrowhead versus transverse terminal pen cap. Literal geometry is secure enough to retain; directedness is not decided.
- Connector of (196): \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1ex]{\draw (0,0)--(1.81,0);\draw (1.8,.15)--(1.82,-.15);}} — Reduced rightward arrowhead versus transverse terminal cap; kept consistent in geometric transcription with (194), not inferred from projection.

- Inner lower connector of (195): \arrow[dl,dash,start anchor=south west,to path={-- (\tikztotarget.north east)\tikztonodes -- ([xshift=-1pt,yshift=2.3pt]\tikztotarget.north east)}] — One-sided arrowhead versus terminal return stroke. - Second argument of T in the second row of (193): \unsure{d'|d} — The high short trace touches the beginning of the closing parenthesis; it may be a separate prime or only the parenthesis entry.
- Punctuation at the end of the final equivalence: \unsure{.|,} — The oblong dark point has a short oblique extension, so point versus comma is unresolved.

### Unresolved places

- opening range, second index after first ≥: \unsure{d_2|d_1} — 2-like retraced digit; 1 remains possible
- cancelled old term in (204): \deleted{\unsure{D_{\Delta_I}|D_{\Delta_r}|D_{I_r}}} — Delta_I, Delta_r or I_r: crossing strokes prevent secure separation of the lower cluster.
- cancelled fragment before La composante: \deleted{$\unsure{D_{d_0,d_r}|D_{\Delta_r,d_r}}$} — d_0,d_r / Delta_r,d_r; first index obscured
- short form before étale fini: \unsure{revêt} — terminal t-like/raised form is crowded; no expansion to revêtement in the text
- cancelled old argument in (205): \deleted{\unsure{[1,d_0]}} — [1,d_0] plausible; first 1/comma crossed
- cancelled old word in the annotation: \deleted{\illegible{1}} — No defensible full word can be recovered under the strike
- detached terminal curved stroke in the annotation: \text{\tikz[x=.07cm,y=.07cm,baseline=0pt]{\draw[line width=.3pt] (0,0) .. controls (2.1,.4) and (2.9,.4) .. (3,2);}} — Possible partial closing parenthesis or bracket; literal curve is certain, interpretation is not
- Apparent second comma after d_1 in La composante tuple: \unsure{,} — A second small descending stroke lies below/right of the clear comma; its independence from the preceding subscript finish remains uncertain.
- Two isolated marks before (205): \unsure{:|à} — An upper oblique tick and lower short mark favor colon; the lower mark could be a very compressed a.

### Unresolved places

- (206), map label: \unsure{0|o} — numeral zero; lower-case o
- (206), first map connector: \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1em]{\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)--(.9,0)--(.9,.13);}} — literal upward terminal turn; reduced rightward arrowhead
- chain after (206), descending connector to 2: \arrow[d,no head,to path={(\tikztostart.south)--([yshift=2pt]\tikztotarget.center)--++(-.045,.04)\tikztonodes}] — one-sided leftward terminal; reduced downward arrowhead
- (207), map connector: \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1em]{\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)--(.9,0)--(.9,-.10);}} — shaft with tiny downward terminal; reduced rightward arrowhead
- after (207), residual baseline: \unsure{c'est} — c’est with retracing/insertion leader; letters partly overwritten, no secure complete alternative
- after (207), raised unit and attachment: \inserted{\unsure{là|sont}} — là, perhaps la if the possible accent is incidental; sont remains a weaker segmentation; exact insertion attachment uncertain
- after (207), clipped right-edge word: \unsure{analytiques|analytiq} — full ending compressed at the edge; visibly curtailed analytiq with no secure terminal point
- (208), small base glyph: \unsure{D_0|d_0} — capital/cursive D with 0; lower-case d with 0; no independent additional subscript d
- (208), map connector: \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1em]{\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)--(.9,0)--(.9,.13);}} — literal upward terminal; reduced rightward arrowhead
- (209), first left parenthesis: \bigl( — opening-parenthesis-shaped surviving curve with retraced upper section; its function and earlier layer are not secure
- (209), fundamental-groupoid signs: \unsure{\Pi_1|\pi_1},\ \unsure{\Pi|\pi} — capital Π leading from the roof and long legs; lower-case π not graphically separable on this page; the bottom-right sign has no 1
- (209), lower-left D: \unsure{D_{d_0,-,d_r}|D^*_{d_0,-,d_r}} — bare retraced D; star embedded in or obscured by the retracing
- (209), bottom connector: \arrow[r,no head,to path={(\tikztostart.east)--([xshift=-2pt]\tikztotarget.west)--++(0,.055)\tikztonodes}] — literal upward terminal turn; reduced rightward head
- (209), lower-right definition label: \unsure{\mathrm{déf}|\mathrm{def}} — déf with compressed accent; unaccented def
- (209), written number: \unsure{209} — 209 leading; initial digit is open because its lower return merges with the parenthesis, no complete alternative number secure
- (209), rough continuation in the upper-left named index: \widetilde\Pi_{d_0\unsure{-}d_r} — one short dash with an ink interruption; two very closely separated stroke fragments remain possible, unlike the clearly spaced adjacent P pair
- (207), word in the G_d annotation: \unsure{\text{torseur}|\text{fibré}|\text{fibrés}} — The tall crossed initial and following joined loops favor torseur, but fibré/fibrés cannot be excluded from the compressed word. No grammatical role decides the reading.
- Final punctuation after (208): \unsure{.|-} — The isolated terminal mark is short, oblique and elongated; point with a pen exit versus short dash is unresolved.

### Unresolved places

- upper qualification, homotopy sign: \Pi_2 — \pi_2
- upper qualification, first D high loop: \circ — 0
- upper qualification, middle of second D index: D^*_{d_0,\unsure{-,|-}d_r} — Dash with or without a second comma; no secure deletion stratigraphy.
- raised annotation after syst. loc.: ? — Unidentified raised curling sign; its independent lower dot is not secure.
- formula number (210): 2\unsure{1|6|0}0 — Outer2 and0 are clear. The middle upright and merged low loop favor1 but admit6 or0.
- (210), connector: \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex,x=1em,y=1em]{\draw[line width=.45pt,line cap=round] (0,0)--(1.15,0)--(1.10,-.08);}} — Short dash with terminal pen return; reduced rightward arrow. The drawing does not choose direction.
- (210), cancelled first index: T^*_{d_0,\unsure{-},d_r}= — The central index dash meets the cancellation; the leading T itself has a recoverable top bar and stem.
- (210), local-system homotopy sign: \Pi_1 — \pi_1
- second qualification, homotopy sign: \Pi_2 — \pi_2
- cancelled coproduct, left-hand group: \widetilde\Pi_{*\Delta_r} — \Pi_{\Delta_r}; the tilde and star-like cluster overlap cancellation traces.
- (211), left-hand retraced group letter: G — B; no separate earlier B or direction of correction is securely recovered.
- (211), right summand letter: B — G
- (211), cancelled summation condition: d_r\geq-\geq d_0 — The first index and inequality details are crossed; the whole condition remains uncertain.
- (211), description's homotopy sign: \Pi_1 — \pi_1

### Unresolved places

- number of the first display: \unsure{212|217} — 212 / 217: the final digit has a short curved top, near-vertical descent and tiny right foot
- Delta in the lower left subscript of the first display: \widetilde\Pi_{\unsure{\Delta}_r} — Delta is the leading final form; earlier or retraced interior/upper strokes cannot be separated as another complete letter
- cancelled left margin: \deleted{\unsure{au fait}} — au fait tentative; letters partly merge with the strike
- cancelled right-hand word after Pi_{Delta_r}: \deleted{\unsure{pourrait|pourra|provient}} — The separate terminal hooked stroke favors pourrait; the crossed middle does not securely exclude pourra or provient.
- live verb, M1295: \unsure{se réduisant|s'identifiant|s'identifie} — The separate initial loop, high mark and following looped d favor se réduisant, but the compressed middle/ending retain s’identifiant and s’identifie.
- abbreviation after simpliciale: \unsure{p.v.|p.r.} — v-shaped letter / reduced r form; expansion is not used to resolve the shape
- Word in the quotation after Notons: \unsure{commodum|mémoire} — The low joined bodies and two taller internal/terminal strokes favor commodum; mémoire remains possible in the compressed joins.
